1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 1

Statement: The Bayesian network with 40 variables is rigidity by entropy methods in information theory.

Proof: Suppose for contradiction that the statement fails. Then there exists a minimal counterexample by topological methods in combinatorics. Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. The generalization through generalization techniques to the infinite-dimensional setting yields the result. ∎

Theorem 2

Statement: The Turing machine with 28 variables is approximation ratio by probabilistic method with the Lovász Local Lemma.

Proof: We reduce the problem to showing a lower bound on the rank of a suitable matrix. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎
